McKernel Rust Migration Overview
Updated: 2026-05-28

Functional dashboard average, arm64 excluded: 96.7%
McKernel-owned core row average, excluding build/host/user/Rocky/arm64: 100.0%
Mechanical LOC audit share: 32.2% Rust implementation, not a progress score
Previous long-running target: added 68 defensible percentage points in
aggregate across McKernel-owned core sections over successive verified,
non-reboot passes.
Latest requested +135 campaign: verified slices have added 135 defensible
aggregate points across functional dashboard sections without boot/reboot
scripts. That campaign target is met; the full Rust port remains incomplete.
Post-campaign 100% work: verified slices have added 248 more broad aggregate
points. The latest x86 page-table setup batch, x86 five-slice memory batch,
x86 virtual-to-physical walk, x86 page-table destroy, x86 clear/set range
side-effect/top-level orchestration, x86 visitor-body sequencing, x86
clear-range body sequencing, host
schedule-process, and procfs
backlog slices are strict-core progress only and do not add broad dashboard
percentage.
The sysfs response-body, packet-dispatch, request-body, init-body,
packet-handler wrapper, request-wrapper, special-create, procfs thread-control,
procfs buffer-chain, procfs request-finalization, procfs answer, and procfs
backlog slices are
strict-core progress only because the broad
procfs/sysfs/xpmem/file-object row is already capped at 100%.
Completed prior +50 continuation: 50 verified aggregate functional points
landed; that adjusted continuation target is complete. Strict-core movement for
this continuation is about +53 points because the slices move real
range-tree, syscall leaf/getresid, page-allocator add/zero/allocation/free-finish/top-level free,
x86 page-table root-lifecycle/prepare-map/set-PTE, futex wake bodies,
pager/memory-control ABI layouts, decimal formatting digit emission, and
CPU-local lifecycle/selection/preempt-counter plus kmalloc free-list ownership, not
helper-only glue.
Latest requested continuation goal: finish the capped 500/500 tracked
sequencing score after the additional +100 request. The tracker is already
at 500/500, so new verified work records post-saturation strict-core ownership
movement instead of increasing the capped broad dashboard score. Older +32/+50
entries below are historical checkpoints, not the current target.
Strict-core movement for this continuation is about +1095 points
because Rust now owns the central `number()` integer/pointer formatting body,
`format_decode()`/`string()` format-token and string-output bodies used by
`vsnprintf()`, and verified signal/futex/ptrace register layout mirrors that
gate future syscall, signal, ptrace, and futex body ownership. Rust also owns
syscall/control-plane layout mirrors for procfs, coredump, CPU mapping, perf,
UTI, time-of-day, sysinfo, and move-pages SMP request bodies, and x86
descriptor, TSS, fxsave/xsave, YMM, LWP, and bound-register save-state layout
mirrors, sysfs create/mkdir/symlink/lookup/unlink/setup request layout
mirrors, plus the `sigaltstack()` syscall body sequencing; sigaltstack
strict-core movement does not add broad dashboard points because the Syscall
Core row is already capped at 100%. Rust also owns sysfs show/store/release
response-body sequencing, sysfs request packet dispatch, and public sysfs
request send/wait/result sequencing; those strict-core movements do not add
broad dashboard points because the procfs/sysfs/xpmem/file-object row is
already capped at 100%. Rust also owns
coredump ELF note/prstatus/prpsinfo and `iovec` layout mirrors, and x86
CPU-local page, kernel context, TLS descriptor, sysfs ops/handle/bitmap,
profile-event, and interval-timer layout mirrors, moving the broad ABI/layout
	row again. Rust also owns the remaining currently scoped ABI/layout mirrors for
	low-level rwlocks, `kref`, rbtree augmentation callbacks, ftrace branch data,
	memobj ops/object state, SysV shared-memory objects and limits, and the XPMEM
	ID/hash/thread-group/segment/access-permit/partition/permission/attachment
object graph.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes mcctrl `do_futex()` top-level body
routing through Rust for futex command decoding, private/shared selection,
timeout copy/validation/conversion, response-pointer publication, `val2`
argument shaping, and lower futex dispatch. This is not scored in
`rust-source-retirement.txt` because `executer/kernel/mcctrl/futex.c` still
compiles for bridge functions, fallback builds, and lower futex side effects.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes allocator public wrapper dispatch for
NUMA-distance node-count lookup, NUMA-distance init node-count dispatch,
free-path NUMA-node bounds selection, allocation-attempt node-count dispatch,
distance-ID node-count dispatch, top allocator public-body node-count dispatch,
dump free-page query node-count dispatch, and query-free interrupt
node-count/body dispatch. This moves eight more
McKernel-owned allocator route points while the
NUMA topology arrays, query-free per-node page accounting, logging, allocation
primitives, dump page-map mutation, and fallback C bodies remain.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes process create/chain side-effect
callback gate routing through Rust for initial address-space CPU-set publication
and chain-thread VM refcount increment dispatch. This moves two more
McKernel-owned core route points while the CPU-set and refcount primitive side
effects remain C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes process create-thread CPU-set
selection/default publication body routing through Rust for requested CPU
validation/logging, default all-CPU fallback selection, and thread/process
CPU-set bit publication. This moves two more McKernel-owned route points while
the CPU-info and logging callback primitives remain C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes process thread allocation
initialization body routing through Rust for create/clone thread zeroing,
refcount publication, and hash/siblings list initialization. This moves three
more McKernel-owned core route points while thread page allocation/free and
refcount primitive callbacks remain C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes allocated process/VM/resource/idle
object zeroing body routing through Rust for create-thread, fork, new
resource-set, process/thread hash, pid1, idle-thread, idle-VM, and idle-process
initialization. This moves eleven McKernel-owned core route points while
allocation/free and later process/VM initialization primitive callbacks remain
C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes process spinlock initialization gate
routing through Rust for process McKernel-fd locks, process-VM page-table locks,
and create/clone thread spin-sleep locks. This moves four more McKernel-owned
route points while the actual spinlock primitive callback remains C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes process-VM initialization body
routing through Rust for memory-range lock initialization dispatch, page-table
spinlock initialization dispatch, VM refcount publication, VM range rb-root
reset, address-space/process pointer publication, exiting-state reset,
NUMA-mask/default-policy initialization, range-cache reset, and TOFU STAG
lock/hash initialization when enabled. This moves another McKernel-owned
`init_process_vm()` cluster while the raw lock/log primitives and C fallback
body remain.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes CPU-local scheduler queue spinlock
initialization gate routing through Rust for idle-time run queue and migration
queue locks. This moves two more direct McKernel-owned process/scheduler init
call sites while the underlying spinlock primitive callback remains C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes process init state/
parent-inheritance body routing through Rust for initial pid/status
publication, parent/ppid-parent publication, credentials, memory-policy fields,
THP-disable state, rlimit inheritance, CPU-set inheritance, and UTI enable
inheritance. This moves another McKernel-owned `init_process()` cluster while
the C fallback body remains for fallback builds.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes process init list/lock/waitq/
refcount body routing through Rust for hash, sibling, ptrace, report-thread,
thread, and child list initialization; update, thread, child, and coredump
rwlock initialization; McKernel-fd spinlock initialization; waitpid wait queue
initialization; process refcount publication; and monitoring-event reset. This
moves another McKernel-owned `init_process()` cluster while the raw lock,
wait-queue, and refcount primitives plus the C fallback body remain.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes process init profile state body
routing through Rust for profile lock initialization dispatch and profile-event
list reset under `PROFILE_ENABLE`. This is conditional build-path coverage; the
raw MCS lock primitive and C fallback body remain.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes clone-thread base/scheduler state
copy body routing through Rust for CPU-set copy, in-kernel state copy, scheduler
policy copy, and scheduler priority copy. This moves two more McKernel-owned
clone-thread field-copy bodies while the surrounding context initialization,
FP register copy, and arch clone primitive behavior remain C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes create/clone thread link, default
scheduler, exit-status, spin-sleep, fork saved-command-line allocation/copy,
fork VM NUMA/region copy, shared-VM attach, sigcommon share/refcount, and
sigaction-copy body routing through Rust. This moves another validated
McKernel-owned process/clone state cluster while allocation, refcount, signal,
and lower VM primitive callbacks remain C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes clone user-context copy plus
stack-pointer/program-counter mutation, fork profile inheritance, on-fork VM
publication/clear, and McKernel-fd struct copy body routing through Rust. This
moves eight more McKernel-owned process/clone route points while the raw
context-modify callback, VM copy primitive, fd duplication callback, fallback C
bodies, and lower lifecycle side effects remain C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes copy-user-range VM-range metadata
copy/refcount setup and PTE-copy argument initialization through Rust. This
moves five more McKernel-owned fork VM route points while allocation, rb-tree
insertion, page-table walking, PTE copy/set side effects, rollback traversal,

C-owned.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes copy-user-PTE page-buffer copy/zero

Post-cap strict movement also now includes split shared-memobj page-update

Post-cap strict movement also now includes split-range allocation/init body

Post-cap strict movement also now includes split-range publish body routing

Post-cap strict movement also now includes default VM-range memobj ref/unref
direct routing through Rust for fork range metadata copy, split-range publish,

remaining default process/VM range call sites while page refcounts and fallback
bodies remain pending.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes `sync_process_memory_range()` body
routing through Rust for page-table lock/unlock, non-zerofill memobj

Post-cap strict movement also now includes `remap_process_memory_range()` body

Post-cap strict movement also now includes `invalidate_process_memory_range()`

Post-cap strict movement also now includes `invalidate_one_page()` body routing
through Rust for PTE null/file-offset early exits, physical-page lookup
dispatch, page-offset/file-offset replacement selection, PTE exchange, TLB

boundaries and TLB primitive, memobj invalidate
implementation, panic primitive, page-hash/log callbacks, and fallback C body

Post-cap strict movement also now includes `page_unmap()` public result/log

Post-cap strict movement also now includes `page_map()` public inline body

Post-cap strict movement also now includes `page_is_in_memobj()` and

Post-cap strict movement also now includes update-page-table and

Post-cap strict movement also now includes `access_ok()` public wrapper routing
through Rust for body dispatch and denied-access log callback sequencing. The
address-space hold/release/detach public wrappers are now code-aligned and

in an earlier address-space wrapper checkpoint, so this update only adds the
new `access_ok()` public wrapper point.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes direct Rust refcount primitive
mutation for the default process/VM/thread/address-space/sigcommon lifecycle
paths. `process_ref_inc_result()`, `process_ref_set_result()`, and
`process_ref_dec_and_test_result()` now perform Rust-owned atomic mutation when
the default path passes no C callback, and the C call sites for create, clone,
hold, release, and shared-sigcommon paths use that direct route. This moves
eighteen more McKernel-owned route points while memobj/page refcount
primitives, allocation/free, lock primitives, and fallback C bodies remain.

Post-cap strict movement also now includes scheduler priority syscall wrapper
body routing for `sched_get_priority_max()` and `sched_get_priority_min()`
through Rust-owned body-result helpers with C fallback mirrors. The simple
`SYSCALL_DECLARE()` wrapper audit now finds no wrappers without `_result(` in
their textual body, but this is a narrow routing audit rather than proof that
top-level syscall dispatch, argument extraction, scheduler internals, or C
fallback bodies are retired. This adds no broad dashboard percentage because
the Syscall core and Scheduler/timers/wait/futex rows are already capped at
100%.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes stopped-child wait body routing
through `wait_stopped_body_result()`: Rust owns stopped source selection,
status publication, thread/process/main-thread exit-status reap callback
dispatch, and PID/TID return shaping. C still owns the wait scans, locks,
user-copy, rusage, logging, and fallback body. This adds no broad dashboard
percentage because the Syscall core row is already capped at 100%.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes thread-exit signal reporting through
`thread_exit_signal_body_result()`: Rust owns report-parent gating, signal
selection, SIGCHLD siginfo construction, TSC-to-jiffy callback sequencing,
`do_kill` callback dispatch, log callback dispatch, and waitpid wake callback
dispatch. C still owns the primitive signal delivery, waitqueue wake, time
conversion, logging, and fallback body. This adds no broad dashboard percentage
because the Syscall core row is already capped at 100%.
Post-cap strict movement also now includes `finalize_process()` non-pid1
parent notification through `finalize_process_parent_notify_body_result()`:
Rust owns parent presence validation, termsig-to-notify gating, SIGCHLD siginfo
construction, process utime/stime jiffy callback sequencing, `do_kill` callback
dispatch, optional log callback dispatch, waitpid wake callback dispatch, and
return shaping. C still owns final status mutation, update-lock ordering,

QEMU boot-path preflight: `qemu-system-x86_64` is available through the Rocky
`qemu-kvm` binary, libvirt/image/debug tooling is present, the system libvirt
connection responds, and host `/dev/kvm` is present when commands run outside
the default sandbox. The default sandbox does not expose `/dev/kvm`, and a
non-system `virsh` connection failed there because `/run/user/1000/libvirt` is
read-only. The repository has `scripts/qemu-mckernel-guest.sh` as a disposable
guest boundary for an existing Rocky/RHEL-family qcow2 image, but no real QEMU
McKernel boot has been proven yet. QEMU boot validation should use
`--accel auto` or `--accel tcg` inside the sandbox, and can use KVM only when
the command is run in a context where `/dev/kvm` is exposed; a 2026-05-25
non-boot probe showed unprivileged sandboxed QEMU still fails KVM init, while
an escalated/root QEMU process can initialize KVM and then be killed by timeout
without booting a guest.
The latest kmalloc-body pass also confirmed `qemu-system-x86_64 --version`,
observed `/dev/kvm`, validated `scripts/qemu-mckernel-guest.sh --help`,
validated runner dry-run command construction with
`/tmp/mckernel-qemu-kmalloc.xEKd3q/base.qcow2`, and ran a bounded TCG
boot-process probe against that disposable blank qcow2; it reached SeaBIOS/iPXE
and timed out cleanly without booting McKernel or touching host reboot paths.
The latest kmalloc-tracking pass repeated those safe checks with
`/tmp/mckernel-qemu-track.mX1nMd/base.qcow2`; a raw bounded probe exited 124
because `timeout` terminated the blank-image boot as expected, and the wrapped
rerun treated that expected timeout as a passing QEMU boot-boundary probe.
The pagealloc-tracking pass repeated the safe dry-run and bounded TCG probe
with `/tmp/mckernel-qemu-pagealloc.O8Ur9D/base.qcow2`; the allocator
tracking/lifecycle pass repeated them with
`/tmp/mckernel-qemu-lifecycle.mAxKdJ/base.qcow2`. Both blank-image probes
timed out cleanly under the wrapper without booting McKernel or touching host
reboot paths.
The wait-zombie/reparent syscall pass repeated the safe dry-run and bounded
TCG probe with `/tmp/mckernel-qemu-waitzombie.vDIyU2/base.qcow2`; the blank
image timed out waiting for SSH as expected without booting McKernel or
touching host reboot paths.
Current total distance to 100: 40 aggregate functional points across the
non-arm64 dashboard rows, or 0 points across the McKernel-owned core rows
excluding build/host/user/Rocky/arm64.
Standalone Rust target: tracked in `migration.txt` as a stricter end-state than
strict core ownership. Current standalone readiness is about 40%; it reaches
100 only when the default x86_64/Rocky kernel and required control-plane path no
longer depend on McKernel-owned C implementation or fallback bodies below the
external/user-code boundary.

How to read this number
------------------------------------------------------------

This is a functional-area estimate, not a strict line-count. For this goal, the
functional dashboard is the progress score: core categories need to reach 100%
with verified Rust-owned behavior. The mechanical LOC report is retained only
as a C-debt inventory and sanity check; it does not measure whether the port is
functionally close to done.

Current Progress Baseline
------------------------------------------------------------

Use this initial dashboard to track the migration:

Area                                      Rust %   Status
----                                      ------   ------
Rust build/link foundation                   95   Rust object builds and links into mckernel.img; user tools link Rust helper objects; IHK Linux-module Kbuild now links Rust helper objects into `ihk`, `ihk-smp-x86_64`, and `mcctrl`; Rust-linked IHK module-load smoke now passes; V10 minimal smoke and focused V11 strace previously repeated cleanly on Rocky

Latest Rocky/IHK strict-readiness closeout
------------------------------------------------------------

The strict standalone-readiness tracker in `migration.txt` now records
`Rocky/IHK control-plane implementation` as 85% -> 100% (+15). Rust owns the
default-path master IKC init/finalize result sequencing, host master-channel
bring-up ordering, Linux IKC work scheduling/extraction, send-interrupt
dispatch, OS boot kmsg/notifier/master-init rollback sequencing, and OS
shutdown status/wait/thaw/NMI/notifier/finalize/shutdown/kmsg-release
sequencing. C remains for public ABI wrappers, explicit fallback builds, raw
Linux/IHK primitive side effects, lock/list primitive implementations, device
allocation, memory registration, and file I/O boundaries.

Validation for the closeout used only non-reboot gates: Rust formatting,
equivalence coverage including the IHK module helper smoke, Rust-enabled
`mckernel.img` plus `ihk`, `ihk-smp-x86_64`, and `mcctrl` module builds, C
fallback builds for the same targets, shell syntax checks, whitespace checks,
and the dashboard consistency report. No host reboot, host `mcreboot.sh`, or
reboot-capable Rocky script was run.

Previous XPMEM close/dup/flush lifecycle strict-core batch
------------------------------------------------------------

Previous strict-core-only XPMEM/file-object batch moved XPMEM
`close()`/`dup()`/flush lifecycle sequencing through Rust while preserving the
C ABI callbacks, primitive atomics, primitive locks, release/destroy callback
surface, scalar no-SIMD Rust code generation, and C fallback scaffolding:

- `dup()` clears the duplicated mckfd data field and increments the partition
  open count through the primitive atomic callback.
- `close()` decrements the partition open count, logs the count, selects flush
  and partition-exit actions, calls the flush callback only for live XPMEM
  data, and calls partition exit only when the open count reaches zero.
- `flush()` finds the target group under the partition hash-list lock, unlinks
  it from the hash list, publishes destroy/destroyed flags under the group
  spinlock, orders access-permit and segment release callbacks, and dispatches
  the final destroy callback.
- Equivalence coverage now includes duplicate data clearing, close-with-flush,
  close-with-exit/no-flush, missing target-group flush, successful target-group
  flush, lock/list/spin callback ordering, flag publication, and no-SIMD/
  unresolved-symbol checks.

through Rust while preserving the C ABI wrapper, typed primitive callbacks, and
C fallback scaffolding:

- Waitqueue entry initialization, prepare, and finish callback ordering.
- Process and thread scan dispatch gates using the existing `wait_proc()` and
  `wait_thread()` C scan callbacks.
- No-child, `WNOHANG`, pending-signal interrupt, schedule handoff, wake/rescan,
  and wait-log sequencing.
- Equivalence coverage for immediate process hit, clone/thread scan, empty
  child set, `WNOHANG`, pending signal before sleep, sleep/rescan/found, and
  invalid callback/null cases.

The Syscall core strict ownership row moved 87% -> 88% in `migration.txt`, and
post-+50 strict-core movement was about +201 points. The broad Syscall core
dashboard row was already capped at 100%, so this does not add broad dashboard
percentage. C still owns child/thread list traversal and mutation inside
`wait_proc()`/`wait_thread()`, waitqueue/schedule/signal primitive behavior,
rusage aggregation, release/detach side effects, syscall dispatch, user-copy
primitives, and fallback scaffolding.

Current completed Rust-owned behavior
------------------------------------------------------------

- Freestanding no_std Rust kernel crate exists under kernel/rust/.
- Rust can be linked into the x86_64 mckernel.img path.
- C fallback builds remain part of the migration contract.
- Rust exports C ABI replacements for the first rbtree, llist, and bitmap
  routines.
- Rust now owns the exported bit-scan and hweight helper cluster from
  `lib/bitops.c`: `find_next_bit()`, `find_next_zero_bit()`,
  `find_first_bit()`, `find_first_zero_bit()`, `__sw_hweight8()`,
  `__sw_hweight16()`, `__sw_hweight32()`, and `__sw_hweight64()`.
- Rust now owns the external string/memory leaf helper cluster from
  `lib/string.c` when ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is on: `strlen()`, `strnlen()`,
  `strcpy()`, `strncpy()`, `strcmp()`, `strncmp()`, `strchr()`,
  `strrchr()`, `strpbrk()`, `strstr()`, `memcpy()`, `memcpy_long()`,
  `memset()`, and `memcmp()`. The allocation/user-copy dependent
  `flatten_strings_from_user()` body remains in C.
- Rust now owns the priority-list add/delete core from `kernel/plist.c`:
  `plist_add()` and `plist_del()`. This keeps futex queue ordering logic
  equivalent while leaving futex wakeup, locking, and scheduler side effects in
  C.
- Rust now owns the pure waitqueue list-manipulation core from
  `kernel/waitq.c`: `waitq_init()`, `waitq_add_entry_locked()`,
  `waitq_remove_entry_locked()`, and `waitq_wake_nr_locked()`. Scheduler
  callbacks, spinlock wrappers, current-thread state changes, wakeup wrappers,
  timers, and futex behavior remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the small init/memory query helper cluster:
  `find_command_line()` from `kernel/init.c`, plus
  `is_mckernel_memory()` and `phys_to_nid()` from `kernel/mem.c`. The page
  address validator, page-table logic, mapping paths, and high-risk memory
  management behavior remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the page query/splitability leaf helper cluster from
  `kernel/mem.c`: `page_to_phys()` and `is_splitable()`. The Rust side has
  direct `struct page` layout assertions, while page hash lookup, page-table
  walking, mapping, and memory-object operations remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the SysV shared-memory ID helper cluster from
  `kernel/syscall.c`: `get_shmid_max_index()`, `get_shmid_index()`,
  `make_shmid()`, `shmid_to_index()`, and `shmid_to_seq()`.
- Rust now owns the SysV shared-memory permission and lock-limit policy helper
  cluster from `kernel/syscall.c`: `shmget_existing_access_result()`,
  `shmat_access_result()`, `shmctl_ipc_stat_access_result()`,
  `shm_owner_result()`, `shm_owner_or_cap_result()`, and
  `shmlock_rlimit_result()`. Object lookup, reference handling, locking,
  user-copy, memory mapping, and the remaining shm syscall orchestration remain
  C-owned.
- Rust now owns the scheduler priority policy leaf helpers from
  `kernel/syscall.c`: `sched_get_priority_max_value()` and
  `sched_get_priority_min_value()`. The syscall wrappers still extract
  arguments in C, and scheduler/thread lookup behavior remains C-owned.
- Rust now owns additional scheduler policy and affinity helpers from
  `kernel/syscall.c`: `sched_policy_is_valid()`,
  `sched_policy_needs_root()`, `setscheduler_validate()`,
  `sched_rr_interval_nsec()`, `sched_affinity_permission_result()`,
  `sched_getaffinity_len_result()`, and `sched_affinity_copy_len()`. Thread
  lookup, Linux permission forwarding, affinity mask mutation, migration
  requests, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns `prlimit64` resource policy helpers from `kernel/syscall.c`:
  `prlimit_validate_resource()`, `prlimit_validate_new_limit()`,
  `prlimit_linux_update_needed()`, and `prlimit_to_mckernel_resource()`.
  Process lookup, rlimit storage, lock ownership, user-copy, and Linux
  forwarding remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns syscall policy and VM range validation helpers from
  `kernel/syscall.c`: `robust_list_len_result()`, `tkill_tid_result()`,
  `sigaction_validate()`, `setpgid_normalize_pid()`,
  `setpgid_normalize_pgid()`, `setpgid_execed_result()`,
  `memlock_prepare_range()`, `memlock_range_flag_result()`,
  `range_has_disallowed_change_flags()`, `munmap_prepare_range()`, and
  `mprotect_prepare_range()`. Signal/action state, process lookup, VM range
  tree mutation, page-table changes, locking, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns additional memory-syscall and NUMA-policy validation helpers
  from `kernel/syscall.c`: `mlockall_policy_result()`,
  `remap_file_pages_prepare()`, `mremap_prepare_args()`,
  `mremap_fixed_range_result()`, `mremap_maymove_result()`,
  `msync_prepare_range()`, `msync_locked_range_result()`,
  `mbind_prepare_range()`, `mempolicy_nodemask_bits_result()`,
  `mempolicy_nodemask_bits_is_clamped()`, `mbind_mode_flags_result()`,
  `mempolicy_mode_is_supported()`, `set_mempolicy_normalize_mode()`,
  `get_mempolicy_validate()`, and `move_pages_policy_result()`. Mapping
  mutation, NUMA mask copy/verification, migration allocation, page-table
  updates, and range-tree ownership remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns additional `brk()`, `mincore()`, and `do_mmap()` policy
  helpers from `kernel/syscall.c`: `brk_prepare_result()`,
  `brk_default_vrflags()`, `mincore_prepare_range()`,
  `mmap_base_vrflags()`, `mmap_populated_mapping_result()`,
  `mmap_should_set_host_ro()`, `mmap_update_private_maxprot()`,
  `mmap_prot_denied_result()`, `mmap_maxprot_to_vrflags()`,
  `mmap_should_force_straight()`, and `mmap_is_shared()`. Free-space search,
  memory-object creation, physical page allocation, host VMA updates, range
  insertion, prefaulting, and mapping teardown remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns additional signal/time syscall policy helpers from
  `kernel/syscall.c`: `tgkill_target_result()`,
  `rt_sigprocmask_validate()`, `rt_sigprocmask_apply()`,
  `rt_sigpending_size_result()`, `signalfd4_sigsetsize_result()`,
  `signalfd4_flags_result()`, `getrusage_who_result()`,
  `itimer_which_result()`, `itimer_is_real()`, `itimer_should_start()`,
  `clock_gettime_dispatch()`, `gettimeofday_dispatch()`, and
  `nanosleep_validate_timespec()`. Signal queue ownership, user-copy, actual
  timer state updates, CPU-time accounting walks, and Linux forwarding remain
  C-owned.
- Rust now owns additional signal-wait and alternate-signal-stack policy
  helpers from `kernel/syscall.c`: `rt_sigtimedwait_prepare()`,
  `rt_sigtimedwait_timeout_result()`, `rt_sigtimedwait_prepare_masks()`,
  `rt_sigtimedwait_deadline()`, `rt_sigtimedwait_timeout_expired()`,
  `sigmask_to_signal_number()`, `rt_sigqueueinfo_pid_result()`,
  `sigsuspend_sigsetsize_result()`, `sigsuspend_prepare_mask()`,
  `sigsuspend_pending_matches()`, `sigaction_sigsetsize_result()`,
  `sigaltstack_validate()`, and `sigaltstack_is_disable()`. Pending-signal
  list traversal, signal delivery, signal-action storage, thread status
  transitions, alternate-stack storage updates, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns additional ptrace and process-vm policy helpers from
  `kernel/syscall.c`: `process_vm_validate_args()`,
  `process_vm_op_is_write()`, `process_vm_op_is_valid()`,
  `ptrace_signal_data_result()`, `ptrace_detach_signal_result()`,
  `ptrace_user_area_result()`, `ptrace_status_allows_io()`,
  `ptrace_setoptions_flags_result()`, `ptrace_apply_options()`,
  `ptrace_child_traced_result()`, `ptrace_attach_policy_result()`,
  `ptrace_detach_state_result()`, and `ptrace_siginfo_state_result()`. Process
  lookup, ptrace attach/detach state mutation, register access, remote memory
  reads/writes, cross-process VM copying, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns wait4/waitid process/thread wait policy helpers from
  `kernel/syscall.c`: `wait4_options_result()`,
  `waitid_to_wait_pid_result()`, `waitid_options_result()`,
  `wait_should_scan_process_result()`, `wait_should_scan_thread_result()`,
  `wait_process_pid_matches_result()`, `wait_thread_tid_matches_result()`,
  `wait_process_exited_candidate_result()`,
  `wait_thread_exited_candidate_result()`,
  `wait_nonptraced_stop_candidate_result()`,
  `wait_ptraced_stop_candidate_result()`,
  `wait_continued_candidate_result()`, `wait_reap_needed_result()`,
  `wait_nohang_result()`, `wait_empty_result()`,
  `wait_stopped_status_result()`, `wait_continued_status_result()`,
  `wait_zombie_skip_host_result()`, `wait_thread_empty_candidate_result()`,
  and `waitid_status_code_result()`. Child-list traversal/mutation,
  reparenting, rusage aggregation, signal delivery, ptrace detach mutation,
  host wait cleanup, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns clone, execveat, ptrace-event, and futex command policy
  helpers from `kernel/syscall.c`: `clone_pthread_marker_result()`,
  `clone_flags_result()`, `clone_host_parent_flags_result()`,
  `clone_report_thread_result()`, `ptrace_exec_event_signal_result()`,
  `ptrace_syscall_event_signal_result()`, `ptrace_clone_event_result()`,
  `ptrace_clone_reparent_result()`, `execveat_policy_result()`,
  `futex_decode_flags_result()`, `futex_wait_timeout_needed_result()`,
  `futex_timeout_is_absolute_result()`, `futex_clock_id_result()`,
  `futex_requeue_val2_result()`, and `futex_timeout_ns_result()`. Actual
  thread/process allocation, ptrace report mutation, exec image loading,
  futex queues, futex wake/requeue internals, Linux clock/syscall fallback,
  and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns XPMEM policy helpers from `kernel/xpmem.c`:
  `xpmem_make_initial_policy_result()`, `xpmem_make_alignment_result()`,
  `xpmem_get_policy_result()`, `xpmem_perms_result()`,
  `xpmem_check_permit_mode_result()`, `xpmem_validate_access_result()`, and
  `xpmem_attach_overlap_result()`.
- Rust now owns additional XPMEM ID, hash, owner, attach/detach, and
  destroying-state policy helpers from `kernel/xpmem.c` and
  `kernel/include/xpmem_private.h`: `xpmem_id_to_tgid_result()`,
  `xpmem_tg_hashtable_index_result()`,
  `xpmem_ap_hashtable_index_result()`, `xpmem_make_id_result()`,
  `xpmem_positive_id_result()`, `xpmem_owner_policy_result()`,
  `xpmem_attach_initial_policy_result()`, `xpmem_destroying_state_result()`,
  `xpmem_is_destroying_result()`, `xpmem_destroying_error_result()`,
  `xpmem_two_destroying_error_result()`,
  `xpmem_three_destroying_error_result()`,
  `xpmem_attach_destroying_result()`, and
  `xpmem_detach_lookup_result()`. XPMEM object allocation, lock ownership,
  refcount mutation, list mutation, remap/page-table updates, and user-copy
  remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns additional XPMEM range and page-table decision helpers from
  `kernel/xpmem.c`: `xpmem_remove_range_step_result()`,
  `xpmem_remove_memory_range_action_result()`,
  `xpmem_range_private_invalid_result()`,
  `xpmem_clear_pte_range_result()`, `xpmem_fault_vaddr_result()`,
  `xpmem_straight_phys_result()`, `xpmem_remote_pte_missing_result()`,
  `xpmem_seg_phys_plus_off_result()`, `xpmem_att_page_fits_result()`,
  `xpmem_pte_mismatch_result()`, and `xpmem_unpin_step_result()`. Range
  splitting/freeing, attachment list mutation, page-table mutation, PTE
  installation, pin/unpin accounting mutation, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns additional XPMEM refcount/object-list and destroy-state
  decision helpers from `kernel/xpmem.c`:
  `xpmem_close_decision_result()`,
  `xpmem_ref_drop_should_free_result()`,
  `xpmem_begin_destroy_result()`, `xpmem_finish_destroy_result()`, and
  `xpmem_object_lookup_decision_result()`. Atomic increments/decrements,
  kfree, lock ownership, list mutation, attachment/remap/page-table mutation,
  and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the first file/dev/procfs/sysfs object decision helper batch
  behind the existing C ABI through `kernel/rust/object_helpers.rs`:
  file-object hash/mode/refcount keep/base-flag/status/premap/page-I/O/flush
  decisions; device-object page-count, PFN-table, PFN-cache, PFN-present,
  physical-address, and range-index decisions; sysfs path validation,
  special-op classification, string-bit sizing, and response-error mapping;
  and procfs memory-fault reason, chunk sizing, pagemap range, process-status
  state, and thread-stat state selection. C fallback builds use
  `kernel/object_helpers.c`. Allocation, locks, list mutation, page-table
  mutation, page I/O, sysfs IKC exchange, procfs buffer mutation, and user-copy
  remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the second object-helper decision batch behind the same C ABI:
  pager EINTR retry, scratch allocation sizing, copy-size validation,
  fault-address masking, and read chunk sizing; zeroobj initial flags/refcount
  and page validation; SysV shmobj pgshift/pgsize, flag, real-size,
  page-containment, destroy-index, get/lookup-page, and update-page address
  decisions; and hugefile object p2align, page-index, per-page count, pgsize,
  initial status/refcount, grow/copy/zero sizing decisions. Allocation,
  refcount mutation, locks, list mutation, page-table mutation, page
  allocation/free, user-copy, and object lifetime mutation remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the third object/procfs/sysfs/dev/file decision batch behind
  the same C ABI: fileobj initial ref/sref, premap NUMA-node wrap, pages-byte
  sizing, premap page indexing, allocation npages/flags/size, pageio pgsize
  and reschedule decisions, and new/mapped page modes; devobj PFN table bytes,
  base flags, initial refcount, PFN request offset, PFN-store decision, and
  map size; sysfs parameter sizing, data buffer sizing, and packet-error
  decisions; and procfs default/remote counts, remote npages, format-error
  validation, locked-KB reporting, maps permission chars, maps path kind,
  pagemap next position, and auxv limit. Allocation, refcount mutation, locks,
  lists, page tables, sysfs IKC exchange, procfs buffer mutation, and user-copy
  remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the fourth object/procfs/sysfs/dev/file decision batch behind
  the same C ABI: fileobj path-present, invalid page-count, hashed-page free,
  premap page-present, and lookup-miss decisions; devobj path/PFN-table
  presence and mapped-PFN composition; sysfs request-busy, handle-pointer,
  default response, release-response, and request-handler dispatch decisions;
  and procfs release-message, root match, OS-number match, zero-length read,
  locked-size accumulation, and bitmask offset decisions. C still owns the
  actual allocation/free calls, reference mutation, locks, lists, page-table
  mutation, sysfs IKC sends, procfs buffer mutation, and user-copy.
- Rust now owns the fifth object/pager decision batch behind the same C ABI:
  pager arealist tail-room/count math, addrpair sizing, file-position math,
  arealist write-result validation, Linux-mlock sentinel handling,
  mlock-container movement decisions, pagein data-position math, pageout
  argument validation, anonymous-range skip policy, locked-range detection,
  physical-removal skip policy, fd-valid checks, swap-unlink decisions, and
  short-I/O errno mapping. The pageout/pagein orchestration, page-table
  mutation, Linux syscall forwarding, allocation/free calls, and user-copy
  remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the sixth object/procfs/sysfs/shmobj/hugefile decision batch
  behind the same C ABI: sysfs allocation-present and callback-present checks;
  procfs remote-buffer, backlog-vs-EAGAIN, task TID, pointer-present, and
  buffer-chain attach decisions; SysV shm/shmlock user matching, lock-release,
  page-count, direct-vs-indexed destroy, destroy-flag, initial object state,
  page allocation, new-page state, lookup-miss, PTE-miss, and update-loop
  decisions; and hugefile pointer/page presence, page-array sizing, and
  zero-tail indexing. Allocation/free calls, list mutation, locks, page-table
  mutation, IKC exchange, procfs buffer copies, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the seventh shared memobj/page decision batch behind the same
  C ABI: memobj unref-free decisions, operation-pointer presence checks,
  missing/default page-operation results, pager/removable flag predicates,
  flushable page/object predicates, freeable XPMEM attachment filtering,
  remap-file-pages eligibility, page mode-in-memobj classification, and
  multi-mapped page classification. Atomic reference mutation, indirect
  memobj operation calls, allocation/free orchestration, locks, list mutation,
  page-map/page-table mutation, and user-copy remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the eighth procfs/pager/file decision batch behind the same
  C ABI: procfs entry-name classification, procfs `comm` basename selection,
  pager Linux read/write loop stop/advance/remaining/complete decisions,
  pager copy-fault retry/error mapping, file-object shared-reference
  increment math, and file premap NUMA-interleave policy. Linux syscall
  forwarding, procfs buffer mutation, page-fault execution, user-copy,
  file-object lifetime mutation, allocation/free, locks, lists, and page-table
  mutation remain C-owned.
- Rust now owns the numeric parser leaf helpers from `lib/vsprintf.c`:
  `simple_strtoul()`, `simple_strtol()`, `simple_strtoull()`,
  `simple_strtoll()`, `strict_strtoul()`, `strict_strtol()`,
  `strict_strtoull()`, `strict_strtoll()`, and `strtol()`. Varargs
  formatting and scanning remain in C.
- Rust now owns bitmap hex/list parser helpers from `lib/bitmap.c`:
  `__bitmap_parse()`, `bitmap_parse_user()`, `bitmap_parselist()`, and
  `bitmap_parselist_user()`. Bitmap string/list printing stays in C because it
  depends on `scnprintf()` formatting.
- Rust now owns bitmap_find_next_zero_area(), bitmap_find_free_region(),
  bitmap_allocate_region(), and bitmap_release_region() when
  ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is on, while the C implementations remain available for
  fallback builds.
- Rust now owns the first page-allocator rbtree helper cluster
  `__page_alloc_rbtree_free_range()` and
  `__page_alloc_rbtree_alloc_pages()` when ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is on, while the
  C helper implementations remain available for fallback builds.
- Rust now owns the generic bitmap-backed page allocator no-lock helper
  cluster when ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is on:
  `__ihk_pagealloc_large_nolock()`, `__ihk_pagealloc_alloc_nolock()`,
  `__ihk_pagealloc_reserve_nolock()`, `__ihk_pagealloc_free_nolock()`,
  `__ihk_pagealloc_count_nolock()`, and
  `__ihk_pagealloc_query_free_nolock()`. The exported C page allocator API and
  MCS lock/IRQ handling remain in C wrappers, and C fallback builds still use
  the original C implementation.
- Rust now owns the no-lock zero-free helper
  `__ihk_pagealloc_zero_free_pages_nolock()` behind the C exported
  `__ihk_pagealloc_zero_free_pages()` lock/log wrapper.
- Rust now owns the rbtree reserve/get-root helper pair:
  `__page_alloc_rbtree_reserve_pages()` and
  `__page_alloc_rbtree_get_root_chunk()`.
- Rust now owns the `__ihk_numa_add_free_pages()` helper body behind the
  exported C logging wrapper, including incoming range zeroing, rbtree
  insertion, min/max tracking, and free-page accounting.
- Rust now owns the single-node deferred-zero helper
  `__ihk_numa_zero_free_pages_node()` while the C wrapper still owns the
  all-node traversal and zero-at-free policy check.
- Rust now owns the under-lock NUMA allocation body
  `__ihk_numa_alloc_pages_nolock()` while the exported C
  `ihk_numa_alloc_pages()` wrapper still owns the CPU-local-cache precheck,
  MCS lock/unlock, and debug logging.
- Rust now owns the direct NUMA free-to-tree helper
  `__ihk_numa_free_pages_to_tree_nolock()` while the C wrapper still owns
  bounds checks, zero-now policy, locking, and logging.
- Rust now owns the deferred NUMA free enqueue helper
  `__ihk_numa_defer_zero_free_pages()` while the C wrapper still owns the
  public free orchestration and IKC/Linux zero request path.
- Rust now owns the NUMA free-path preflight helper
  `__ihk_numa_free_pages_prepare()`, including node bounds checks, positive
  page-count filtering, zero-now clearing, and direct-vs-deferred action
  selection. The C wrapper still owns locks, logging, CPU-local-cache handling,
  and the IKC/Linux zero request.
- Rust now owns the process/VM decision-helper batch in `process_helpers.rs`:
  `common_vrflag_to_ptattr()`, `process_split_pgshift_result()`,
  `process_add_range_bounds_result()`, `process_extend_up_result()`,
  `process_change_prot_newflag_result()`, `process_attr_delta_result()`,
  `process_private_file_setattr_result()`,
  `process_remove_region_alignment_result()`,
  `process_access_initial_result()`, `process_access_adjacent_result()`, and
  `process_access_permission_result()`. The C side still owns allocation, rb
  tree insertion/removal, locks, page-table mutation, fault handling, logging,
  and range lifetime.
- Rust now owns x86_64 page-attribute translation helpers in
  `x86_memory_helpers.rs`: `x86_attr_to_l3attr_result()`,
  `x86_attr_to_l2attr_result()`, and `x86_attr_to_l1attr_result()`, plus the
  smaller-page-size selector `x86_smaller_page_size_result()`. The
  `attr_to_l3attr()`, `attr_to_l2attr()`, `attr_to_l1attr()`, and
  `arch_get_smaller_page_size()` wrappers now call the Rust path when
  ENABLE_RUST_KERNEL is on, while C still owns page-table walking, mapping,
  unmapping, TLB-sensitive mutation, and user-copy.
- Rust now owns the first user-tool helper object for `mcstat`: memory
  scale/unit selection, periodic header cadence, monitor-status labels, and
  OS-status labels. The C side still owns IHK library calls, device opens,

  seconds. McKernel now disables the user VDSO on this Rocky path and serves
  `SYS_time` locally from the boot-time/TSC fallback. The boot kmsg records:
  `vdso disabled: host vgtod data unavailable`.

  `PTRACE_SETOPTIONS` masks.

  EDT ptrace/process-vm batch, and prior signal-wait, signal/time,
  mmap/brk/mincore, memory-policy, syscall-policy, scheduler/prlimit, SysV
  shared-memory, page/memory/init-helper, bitops/free-preflight/string/plist/
  numparse/bitmap-parse/waitq-core work is validated through equivalence,
  source hygiene, shell syntax, Rust/C image builds, symbol scan, SIMD scan,
  V10 boot smoke, targeted procfs runtime smoke, host sysfs show smoke,
  targeted SysV shared-memory runtime smoke, targeted scheduler/prlimit
  runtime smoke, targeted syscall-policy runtime smoke, targeted memory-policy
  runtime smoke, targeted mmap/brk/mincore runtime smoke, targeted signal/time
  runtime smoke, targeted signal-wait runtime smoke, targeted ptrace/process-vm
  runtime smoke, and focused XPMEM runtime smoke. Direct XPMEM user runtime is
  no longer blocked on this host when the installed XPMEM module is loaded.
  Direct pager pagein/pageout/swapout, writable sysfs store, hugefile-backed
  mapping, device PFN mapping, and `/proc/PID/mem` runtime coverage are still
  required gates before moving their orchestration, user-copy, Linux
  forwarding, IKC exchange, or page-table mutation out of C.

  fork/ptrace memory pressure, and a missing x86_64 mcexec-options suite.
- The pre-existing dirty `ihk` submodule marker is still present from the local
  Rocky compatibility patch and was not changed while fixing the hostname
  SIGSEGV.
- The Rocky validation script now wraps each V10 smoke command in a timeout so
  future runs stop at the first failing command and print command output plus
  recent McKernel kmsg.
- The first timeout wrapper had a shell-status capture bug: after
  `mcexec-true` failed or timed out, it printed status `0` and continued to
  `mcexec-hostname`. The wrapper now preserves the actual `timeout` status and
  stops at the first failing smoke command.
- V10 smoke can run under `strace` with `--trace-smoke`, and dumps Linux
  process state, Linux dmesg, recent McKernel kmsg, and `mcexec_v10:` handoff
  markers on failure.
- The smoke watchdog default is now 8 seconds. The script uses an explicit
  `setsid`-based watchdog instead of relying on GNU `timeout`, because a stuck
  kernel ioctl can prevent `timeout` from returning cleanly.
- After a V10 hang, the next boot attempt can fail early while destroying the
  previous `/dev/mcos0` OS instance. The validation script now runs
  `mcstop+release.sh -k` before boot-smoke and during watchdog cleanup so stale
  `mcexec` holders are killed before a fresh boot is attempted.
- The V10 watchdog now tracks the direct smoke-command PID instead of a
  `setsid` process group and prints the watched PID immediately. Failure
  diagnostics that can themselves block are bounded with short timeouts.
- There is no current Rocky VM build or module-load blocker after V8.
- The V8 trampoline warning remained after reducing the x86_64 private
  trampoline allocation to one low-DMA page. The persistent GRUB `memmap`
  reservation experiment was unsafe on Rocky/VirtualBox, so the safe default is
  to continue V9 with the Linux-trampoline fallback and no persistent boot-arg
  changes.
- V9 boot-only passed with the Linux-trampoline fallback. The boot log still
  reported `Trampoline area: 0x0` and `error: allocating fp_regs pages`; keep
  watching those messages during the repeat V10 run, but they are not blocking
  the latest minimal workload smoke.
- WSL remains blocked for real boot/runtime smoke testing by host module and
  kernel development tree limitations.
- ihk-smp-x86_64 compatibility against Linux 6.6 needs more work before WSL can
  be considered a runtime validation host.

Recommended runtime platform:
- Rocky Linux VM.

Recommended verification style
------------------------------------------------------------

Run the ladder from plan.txt in order:

V0: Documentation integrity
V1: Source inventory
V2: Rust object only
V3: Rust primitive equivalence
V4: Rust-enabled kernel image
V5: C fallback kernel image
V6: User-space tools
V7: Host modules compile
V8: Module load only
V9: McKernel boot only
V10: Minimal user workloads
V11: Focused regression tests
V12: Phase-specific stress

Stop on the first failing step. Record the failing command, environment, and
error summary in kernel.log before making more changes.

Next migration target
------------------------------------------------------------

Recommended next code phase:
- Expand the remaining McKernel-owned process/thread lifecycle surface, then
  move VM/process lifecycle mutation, rusage aggregation boundaries, or ptrace
  signal-delivery bodies behind narrow Rust ABI wrappers only where synthetic
  equivalence fixtures and targeted runtime coverage can cover them. Continue
  object/file/procfs/sysfs conversion only where runtime coverage can exercise
  the path: file-backed mmap, `/proc/PID/mem`, sysfs create/show/store, device
  PFN mappings, and hugetlbfs-backed mappings. Another bounded helper cluster
  is acceptable if it has direct C/Rust equivalence coverage and does not pull
  allocator/user-copy dependencies into Rust.

Reason:
- The Rocky baseline is clean and the current Rust helper set has C/Rust
  equivalence, full Rust/C image-build validation, boot smoke, procfs smoke,
  and a SysV shm runtime smoke. Keep allocation/free, refcount mutation,
  lock/list mutation, page-table mutation, page I/O, sysfs IKC exchange,
  procfs buffer mutation, and user-copy in C until targeted runtime coverage
  exists for those bodies.

Boot-path verification safety position
------------------------------------------------------------

I did not run boot/reboot-capable validation in this pass. The safe path is not
plain host `mcreboot.sh`; the safe path is now the disposable QEMU guest runner
in `scripts/qemu-mckernel-guest.sh`. That runner has only been statically and
dry-run validated so far: it creates a disposable overlay from an existing
Rocky/RHEL-family qcow2, generates cloud-init SSH access, captures a serial log,
can expose the repo read-only over 9p, refuses host `/dev/*` image paths, and
attempts guest `mcstop+release.sh -k` cleanup when SSH came up. This pass also
checked `qemu-system-x86_64 --version`, confirmed `/dev/kvm` exists, and
validated `scripts/qemu-mckernel-guest.sh --dry-run --accel tcg` command
construction against the existing tiny dry-run qcow2. A real McKernel boot
remains unproven until a Rocky 8.x guest image is supplied or generated and the
install/boot smoke runs inside that guest; no usable Rocky/RHEL-family qcow2 was
available from unprivileged paths in this pass. Until then, safe non-boot modes
remain `scripts/rocky-v11-focused.sh --classify-only`,
`scripts/rocky-v11-focused.sh --build-only`,
`scripts/rocky-v11-focused.sh --skip-boot`, image builds, equivalence tests,
and explicitly approved module-load smoke. A plain
`scripts/rocky-v11-focused.sh` run is not safe by default because it can invoke
`mcreboot.sh`.
